People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really hope I'm not taking up a spot from someone who needs it more, but... um... I guess I'm here because things have gotten a little overwhelming lately. 

I know this is probably a terrible thing to say right off the bat, and I'm sorry to even bring it up, but... I just keep thinking that it might be better if I just didn't wake up in the morning. It's embarrassing to admit that out loud. It's just... I've made things so hard for my family, and I've really let down my flooring crew at work, too. Sometimes I honestly feel like dying would just be the fair punishment for what I've done to all of them. 

I know I shouldn't think that way, and I really am trying to be better about it. I hope it's okay that I came in to talk about this... is that the sort of thing you can help with?

Um... well... I know this probably sounds really strange, and I hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying it. I really hesitate to even bring it up because I know it sounds bad, but I want to be a good patient and give you all the information you need. 

Sometimes... and it's not all the time, I promise... but sometimes I hear a man's voice. It usually happens when I'm at a job site, or even just standing in my own kitchen. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, not really at the same time. I hope it's okay that I can't give you a specific schedule for it. I really try to ignore it, I promise I do. 

It just sort of happens randomly... like when I'm at a job site working on a floor, or even just standing in my own kitchen. I'll hear this man's voice right behind me, clear as day, like he's standing right over my shoulder. He says things like, 'You missed it,' or... or 'Everyone knows.' 

I know it probably sounds crazy, and I'm so sorry, I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind. But it sounds so real that I always turn around to apologize or see who it is, and... nobody's ever there. 

I know I shouldn't let it bother me, and I'm really trying to just focus on my work when it happens. Is... is it really bad that I'm hearing that? I can try harder to tune it out if you think that would be better.

Um... I guess I would have to say it's severely interfering. I'm really sorry to admit that, I know I should be handling things better. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or being weak.

It's just... I actually had to stop leading the flooring installations. I just felt so overwhelmed and confused, and I was so scared I was going to ruin the work or put someone at risk. 

My apprentice and my husband actually had to start stepping in to take over for me before I messed everything up. I feel terrible that they had to do that, making more work for them... I really want to get back to pulling my own weight. Is it... is it okay that I stepped back from leading for a little while? I'm really hoping I can fix this and get back to normal soon.

I've made things so hard for my family, and I've really let down my flooring crew at work, too. Sometimes I honestly feel like dying would just be the fair punishment for what I've done to all of them. \n\nI know I shouldn't think that way, and I really am trying to be better about it. I hope it's okay that I came in to talk about this... is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a while now. Probably about a year and five months? I'm sorry, I should probably know the exact date, but... it started right around the time my husband hurt his back. He's been mostly housebound since then, and... well, I just haven't been handling it very well, which is completely my fault. I should be stronger for him. I hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for? I can try to be more specific if you need me to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really try to keep up with things, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or giving up. But... well, I used to do a lot. I'd paint, cook these big meals, go for a run before my flooring jobs, and I even used to show up for theater rehearsals. \n\nBut lately... and I'm really sorry to admit this, because it sounds terrible... those things just feel like chores now. I hope I'm explaining it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do. I know I probably shouldn't dwell on things, and I really try to just look on the bright side, but... I just feel so awful about everything. \n\nI don't want to sound dramatic, but... I just keep thinking that I'm the reason my husband's back got worse. And I'm the reason my crew lost out on jobs. It's like... I don't know, like I've just messed up clients' homes and poisoned everything I've touched. I really try to be a good person, I promise, but sometimes I just feel like I completely deserve whatever bad things happen to me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I shouldn't be so negative. Is it normal to feel that way? Um... I guess I would have to say it's staying the same, or maybe getting a little worse? I'm sorry, I know I should be making progress by now. I really am trying to stay positive, I promise.
